  10|            The Cooperative Education Program is an extension of the student's technical education that combines classroom
  11|            instruction with on-the-job-training. The Co-op Program provides students with an opportunity to further develop
  12|            academic, technical and employability skills in an industry work environment. All students are eligible to participate
  13|            in the Co-op Program, provided they satisfy state and school grade, attendance and performance requirements. All Co-
  14|            op students have completed the OSHA recognized Career Sate online health and safety course.
  15|             The Cooperative Education Program is beneficial for both student and employer. For the student, it is a chance to
  16|             gain on-the-job training that improves both their technical   and employability skills. Additionally,  making
  17|             connections enhances post-graduate employment opportunities. The Co-op Program allows students to earn while
  18|            Employer benefits include addressing workforce needs in an efficient manner.             Co-op students provide a pool of
  19|            temporary and potential full-time employees who are already trained, thus reducing employer training costs.
  20|             op work hours may be tailored to suit the needs of partnering employers. While overall co-op placements experienced
  21|            a slight decline, due primarily to business closures in response to the recent pandemic, qualified students (Juniors and
  22|            Seniors) continue to work, learn and apply their technical skills in businesses across North Central Massachusetts.
  23|            Student Support Services
  24|            The 2020-2021 school year forced the world to respond to a pandemic with ripple effects to be felt for years to come.
  25|             It goes without saying that we needed to pull together, respond, and unite as partners in order to ensure our special
  26|            populations, and all students regardless of identified disability, remained at the center of decisions. Individuals needed ns. Individuals nectal
  27|            to be met where they were at and all services provided needed to allow for individual needs to be met in new and
  28|            innovative ways. The health and safety of our school community and their families dictated our decisions and actions.
  29|             As the district responded to the unforeseen COVID-19 related closure, the hybrid model, and reopening events that
  30|            occurred throughout the 2020-2021 school year, it did not go unnoticed that our Student Support Service staff,
  31|            students, and families displayed great resilience as they worked together to address individual needs and tackle these
  32|             challenging times. While we hope to never experience these events again, we did share a number of experiences that
  33|             have proven to increase communication and collaboration.       Some of our greatest lessons learned came from this
  34|             experience and we want to extend our sincere gratitude for the efforts put forth during such unprecedented times.
  35|             During the 2020-2021 school year, Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School District provided special
  36|            services to approximately three hundred students - measuring progress of over two hundred students on Individual
  37|             Education Plans (LEPs) and just under one hundred students adhering to individualized Section 504 plans. While the
  38|             Student Support Services (SSS) Department encompasses special education, the department provides support and is
  39|            available to all Monty Tech students.
  40|                                                                         130

===PAGE 132=== (ocr, 42 lines)
   1|            individuals are available for scheduled counseling sessions, mental health emergency treatment, medical/mental health
   2|            re-entry and transition support services, as well as crisis intervention.
   3|             The school's Director of Student Support Services oversees the District's Special Education Program, which is
   4|             reviewed annually in May, in accordance with regulatory requirements. The comprehensive review and evaluation are
   5|             done in collaboration with the Parent Advisory Council, and the results of the evaluation are used to improve the
   6|             special education procedures and programs in place at Montachusett Regional Vocational Technical School.
   7|            During the 2020-2021 Montachusett Regional Technical School District's Department of Student Support Services
   8|             made a concerted effort to increase social, emotional, and community based supports. In addition to our current re-
   9|            entry and school based counseling services, Monty Tech and LUK, Inc. joined forces with the help of the Department
  10|            of Public Health to provide services to at-risk students and their families. Further, the district partnered with LUK to
  11|            create a direct pipeline for Telehealth referrals and access to counseling supports. Couple this with the further
  12|             expansion of our partnership with the SHINE Initiative, it has allowed the district to build upon our mission to provide
  13|             opportunities for all students to be engaged, lead, and access the supports necessary to improve their social and
  14|            emotional wellbeing. The Department of Student Support Services has taken the necessary measures to ensure Social
  15|             Emotional Learning (SEL) is in the forefront of conversations when supporting students as they acclimate to returning
  16|            to school full time after lengthy periods of time experiencing various educational models throughout the pandemic.
  17|             As we establish a full time school routine, we recognize the experiences all students have had over the past few years
  18|            has varied. Courses and activities designed to promote mindfulness, understanding, and social emotional wellness are
  19|            part of the many course offerings. Yoga and Meditative Art, Visual Arts, Directed Studies with Social Emotional
  20|             Learning lessons are all part of the efforts being implemented to ensure we address the needs of all students and
  21|             support their individual growth and development in a post pandemic world. More about tiered interventions can be
  22|             found on our website. However, it is worth noting that we continue to build and refine our services for all students as
  23|             we recognize the importance of developing the whole student and ensuring they are equipped with the necessary to
  24|            become productive citizens that can compete and adapt in a rapidly changing economy.
  25|             Technology
  26|             The 2020-2021 school year proved to be a test in many ways for the Monty Tech educational community, requiring
  27|             1:1 Chromebook initiative, so students and instructors had previous experience with the tools and technology needed
  28|             to learn in a remote or hybrid setting. To support the additional and now required use of this technology, the
  29|             Technology Department spent countless hours during the school year upgrading the network and increasing the
  30|             bandwidth coming into the school to sustain video and audio conferencing.
  31|             Department personnel upgraded the learning experience for students, by purchasing laptops for students and teachers,
  32|             building new iMacs for the Graphic Communications program, and rebuilding surplus computers for full remote
  33|             students in CAD Drafting & Design, Graphic Communications, Business Technology, and Engineering, so that they
  34|             may work from home on their vocational assignments. A ZOOM license was purchased, and the Instructional
  35|             Technologist worked diligently to purchase and deploy software for all teachers. Camcorders, wireless headsets,
  36|             interactive displays and a variety of apps were purchased, installed, and put into the hands of our educators to engage
  37|             learners at all levels.
  38|             Finally, to ensure "business as usual," we invested in Splashtop, VPN client and configured the firewall, so that
  39|             faculty and staff laptops could be fixed remotely if a software issue presented itself, and so that the Business Office
  40|             would have remote access into the school to maintain a secure working environment, even when the building was
  41|             closed.
  42|                                                                         131

===PAGE 133=== (ocr, 41 lines)
   1|            including:
   2|             • Inspector General's inspection results
   3|             • Number of cadets in the program
   4|             • Number of community service hours conducted
   5|            • Number of public affairs events attended by cadets
   6|             • Number of academic awards presented to cadets
   7|            • Participation in drill team competitions, marksmanship competitions, physical fitness competitions, and field
   8|                 trips that support the growth and development of the cadet
   9|             During the 2020 - 2021 school year, MCJROTC Cadets participated in more than 5,000 hours of community service
  10|             and volunteerism. Cadets assisted the Salvation Army by collecting more than $25,000 in donations, which were
  11|             used to countless families in need during the holiday season. These donations will go on to support community
  12|             members that are in need of assistance, especially during the holiday season. day season Tide donation
  13|             The Monty Tech MCJROTC has assisted with color guards and participation in community parades, which provides
  14|             the cadets and community members the opportunity to be involved with civic and veteran ceremonies.
  15|             Finally, the Monty Tech Cadet Corps provided 11 Color Guards for local civic and veteran ceremonies, a highlight
  16|             for students and instructors alike.
  17|             Monty Tech School of Continuing Education
  18|             The Monty Tech School of Continuing Education had an exciting FY21, receiving the program's first-ever training
  19|             grants. School leaders applied for and received Career Technical Initiative Grants (Rounds 1 & 3), totaling $290,000,
  20|             as well as a $30,000 Rapid Reemployment Grant. These grants allowed the School of Continuing Education to
  21|             rollout new training programs, offered to qualified participants (must be unemployed or underemployed) at no charge.
  22|             In addition, each participant who successfully completed the training would be offered job placement with a business
  23|             partner. Comprehensive 200-300 hour programs that culminated in industry-recognized credentials in Welding,
  24|             HVAC and Allied Health were developed and advertised. While the Allied Health (CNA) program did not see
  25|             enough interested participants to field a cohort, HVAC and Welding were successful in their initial endeavor.
  26|             Program leaders continue to track student progress and placements, and expect to pursue additional funding for future
  27|             offerings.
  28|             As we continue to monitor workforce needs and skills gaps, the School of Continuing Education works closely with
  29|             the MassHire North Central Career Center to determine specific training programs that are most in-demand. Our
  30|             overarching goal to support the North Central Massachusetts workforce readiness pipeline with an increase in career
  31|             and licensure courses is evident in new programs that are developed, existing programs that are refined and in
  32|             traditional journeyman/apprenticeship programs that remain a hallmark of our institution.
  33|             The pivot to a hybrid learning model seemed to be beneficial to both our career/licensure students as well as our
  34|             personal enrichment students. During the Fall 2020 and Spring 2021 sessions, we realized the resiliency of our
  35|             students and their willingness to persevere with their training despite the challenges. Our instructors worked
  36|             tirelessly to maintain training programs timelines.
  37|              Our medical students' skill set was and continues to be in high demand due to the pandemic. We were able to
  38|             support our local medical facilities with newly licensed healthcare workers, to include Pharmacy Technicians,
  39|             Medical Billing and Coding Specialists, EKG Technicians, Phlebotomists, and Patient Care Technicians. We hope to
  40|             offer another CNA training program in the future, as the need for trained Assistants remains great.
  41|                                                                         132

===PAGE 134=== (ocr, 44 lines)
   1|            Bridge relationship with Fitchburg State University (FSU). Former graduates have successfully transferred into seats
   2|            in the LPN to BSN Bridge program at SU and will continue their education to a Bachelor's of Science in Nursing
   3|            (BSN). Many class applicants cited their attraction to Monty Tech as the opportunity to complete a ten-month
   4|            accelerated program and then bridge to Fitchburg State University. In the seven -year history of the Bridge Program, all
   5|            of Monty Tech's Practical Nursing graduates reaching graduation at FSU have passed their NCLEX-RN exam. These
   6|            former graduates have gone on to work in hospital settings including Lahey, Beth Israel, and Baystate Medical Center,
   7|            while others have pursued advanced degrees as Critical Care Nurse Practitioners, Nurse Educators, and Family Practice Nurse Practitioners.
   8|            In 2020-2021, Monty Tech Practical Nursing students were asked to complete patient scenarios in the Sim Lab on a
   9|            weekly basis in Terms 2 and 3. The Faculty Sim team has implemented National League of Nursing (NLN) patient
  10|            scenarios and authored several others consistent with the National Council of State Board of Nursing Detailed Test
  11|            Plan. Instructors have also utilized the Sim lab setting to instruct students in developing nursing skills that they may
  12|            not be experiencing in the clinical setting.
  13|            By successfully developing and implementing a vast library of scenarios reflecting INASCAL Standards (International
  14|            Association for Clinical Simulation and Learning), we hope to prepare our students for any experience they may
  15|            encounter as a newly licensed Practice Nurse. In Maternity Sim Lab Boot Camp, for example, students utilize the
  16|            simulation models to imitate the birthing process, from obstetric office visits through labor and common postpartum
  17|            complications. Well newborn care is also taught. Student feedback has been positive regarding these maternity
  18|            scenarios, stating it better prepares them for an unexpected outcome during a delivery. This expanded content also
  19|            better prepares students for NACE (Nursing Acceleration Challenge Exam) success at FSU.
  20|             Substance abuse and the mental health setting job opportunities have increased with more funding being provided to
  21|            combat the opiate crisis. In addition to having specialty clinical rotations, students participate in simulation scenarios
  22|            involving substance abuse and mental health clients, to help to prepare our graduates to work in a substance abuse
  23|            clinical setting.
  24|             Registration in Nursing,
  25|            (Accreditation Commission for Education in Nursing) accreditation to more accurately reflect the changing
  26|            environment in nursing education.
  27|            Looking Ahead
  28|            Undoubtedly, the 2020-2021 school year was challenging and difficult for students and staff, yet the school community
  29|            demonstrated a remarkable resilience and commitment to education and service that proves, yet again, that Monty Tech
  30|            is truly a special place to work and learn. While adapting curriculum to the needs of all students both at home and in-
  31|            person, adhering to state and federal safety and health guidelines, and finding opportunities for students to demonstrate
  32|             their creativity and technical skills, certainly kept staff busy, there was always an element of improvement, vision, and
  33|             forward-thinking that kept moving the school forward. Successful grant applications, new coursework, and increased
  34|             school-to-business partnerships that came about during this difficult year are a few of the more notable initiatives that
  35|             we expect to have a positive impact on our school and students in the coming years:
  36|            Grant funding to improve and expand training programs: Monty Tech has applied for and received six Skills Capital
  37|            Grants, totaling $2,029,686. School officials anticipate a FY22 application may be forthcoming, and as a result, have
  38|            met to determine which program is in need of a shop renovation, updated equipment, and/or new technology that
  39|            reflects current industry standards. The school's Dental Assisting program, which effectively prepares students to enter
  40|            the dental field may be the next vocational program to benefit from grant funding, should an application be approved.: Instructors have advocated for a renovated instructional space, as well as new chairs, tools, and equipment, so that
  41|             students are trained using the exact instruments and technology found in area clinics. This improved facility would
  42|             certainly benefit our high school students, and would also allow Monty Tech's School of Continuing Education to
  43|             develop an all-new evening training program, which would help address the critical shortage for trained Dental
  44|                                                                         133

===PAGE 135=== (ocr, 41 lines)
   1|                                                                                                         VII HULL      VUUVULIULIMI
   2|            program, it may also help feed a struggling STEM workforce pipeline. Whether learning from our industry-immersed
   3|            colleagues, or gaining new instructional resources and techniques from colleagues attending the National Science
   4|            Teachers Association's national conference, Monty Tech science instructors are prepared to deliver new course materials
   5|            that engage students in important and timely science concepts.
   6|            Building partnerships for improved job placement: 2020-2021 brought the school's first-ever approval for a Pre-
   7|            Apprenticeship program - a designation that we expect may improve our graduates' chances of retaining employment
   8|            with a framework for upward mobility. While Welding/Metal Fabrication students already enjoy this new appellation,
   9|            school officials may consider seeking approval for other programs in the coming years. To do so, we must first seek and
  10|            acquire an approved Apprentice Sponsor. This sponsorship and collaboration will serve as a testimony to the strength of
  11|            Monty Tech's vocational programming, and will increase student opportunities for employment and continued education
  12|            and training. Business partners that serve on Program Advisory Boards, as Co-Op employers, or who even graduates
  13|            who now own and operate their own businesses may be interested in such a unique collaboration. In an effort to prepare
  14|            students for careers, it is incumbent on us to ensure that training does not stop when a student leave Monty Tech, and an
  15|            approved apprenticeship would ensure students will have continued support and training as they enter a competitive and
  16|            demanding workforce.

   2|                                                 ANNUAL TOWN MEETING - May 1, 2021
   3|              This was the second annual town meeting with COVID-19 protocols in place. Poll Pads purchased
   4|              through a grant allowed voters to be checked in manually or through a scan of their drivers' license.
   5|              The electronic check in allowed for quick check in as we didn't have to line up by Precinct,
   6|              significantly reducing wait time. Use of the ID (though not required) made the process more efficient
   7|              and safer for all concerned.
   8|              served and led the assembly in the Pledge of Allegiance. The COVID-19 protocols were quickly
   9|              reviewed (microphone directions, use of cards for all votes, no shout outs, etc.). The Moderator then

  11|              Michelle Walton and John Henshaw; and also the current members Terri Burchfield and Heather
  12|              Sroka who are retiring from the committee this year. The Town Manager presented them with
  13|              flowers in appreciation of their service on this committee as well as several other committees in the

  18|             § 250-6.1. Off street parking and loading areas.
  19|             [Amended 5 7 2016 ATM by Art. 39]
  20|             Unless otherwise provided in this bylaw, off street parking and loading spaces shall be required as follows:
  21|                         Dwellings: two parking spaces for each dwelling unit.
  22|                         Place of assembly, including a funeral home: one parking space for each three seats therein or one
  23|             space for each 60 inches of bleachers or benches.
  24|                        Schools: one parking space for each classroom or office, plus one parking space for each three
  25|             seats in the auditorium thereof.
  26|                         Hotels, motels and other places providing overnight accommodations: one parking space for each
  27|             room accommodation therein and adequate spaces for delivery vehicles.
  28|                         Restaurants: one parking space for each table or booth and one parking space for every two
  29|             counter seats and adequate spaces for delivery vehicles.
  30|                         Other service and retail establishments: parking areas, exclusive of driveways, shall be in a
  31|             minimum ratio of three square feet of ground area to one square foot of gross floor area, exclusive of storage
  32|             areas and adequate loading spaces.
  33|                       —Office and all other permitted nonresidential structures, except agricultural: one space for each
  34|             1,000 square feet of gross floor area and loading space for all deliveries and shipping; however, the
  35|             minimum parking area shall be in a minimum ratio of one square foot of ground area for each three square
  36|             feet of floor area.
  37|                        Child care facilities: one parking space for each 10 children in attendance plus one parking space
  38|             for each person engaged on site in the business of the facility at any one time.
  39|                   - For uses not specified above, the number of required spaces will be determined by the Building
  40|             Commissioner based on accepted planning standards, as contained in the latest edition of Time Saver
  41|             Standards for Site Planning, Joseph DeChiara and Lee E. Koppleman, McGraw Hill, New York City, 1984,

   1|            applicability determination.
   2|            (3) Any owner failing to submit the bimonthly report on the prescribed schedule shall have the demolition

   4|            (4) At any time during the delay period, the Historical Commission may advise the Building Commissioner,

   6|                decides that both:

   8|            restore, or move said structure; and
   9|                     B. For at least six months the owner has made continuing bona fide and reasonable efforts to locate a
  10|            purchaser to preserve, rehabilitate, restore, or move the subject building or structure, and that such efforts
  11|            have been unsuccessful.
  12|            § 134-5 Emergency demolition.
  13|            Nothing in this bylaw shall restrict the Building Commissioner or the Fire Chief from ordering the demolition

  28|            more than one, of the following:
  29|                 A. a listing of the building with a licensed real estate brokers;
  30|                 B. an estimate of rehabilitation or restoration costs of the building or structure or;;
